Following Evidence About the Same Part:
Supplementary Protocols and Complete Question Index
Anonymous submission
1
Scope and Reading Guide
Analysis: ldraw2-evidence-v1. Source release: brickatlas-ldraw-2. All 617 original questions, 24 sources and
15,334 placed instances are retained. The main paper reports a method/resource contribution and measured algorithmic controls.
No validated learned-model or human study results are available. “–” denotes not run and is distinct from measured zero.
Sections 2–9 document measurement, comparisons, independent checks, related protocols and reproducibility. The subse-
quent source dossiers reproduce every original English question, option and reference output, with source attribution, operand
mapping, derivation and review status. They are evaluator material. They must not be provided to an inference model. The
compact public-evidence summaries in a dossier do not replace exact model request bodies.

Binding.
Each pair retains pair, parent and source IDs; source and dataset hashes; model revision, adapter and prompt hashes;
condition A/B; both exact requests; canonical predictions; both golds and provenance; truth policy; and explicit failure reasons.
A result pair cannot be formed merely because two ﬁles share a task ID. A missing response becomes a failed observation; it
never shrinks the denominator.
Fact change.
Wrong-image binds gold B to the stored mismatch.alternateChoiceId and veriﬁes its relation to the
B request. Graph-intervention recomputes gold from each supplied graph. The original source-gold wrong-image score is
preserved in a separate ﬁeld. The primary new-gold rate and both-correct rate use every declared pair. Conditional adaptation
has an explicitly displayed A-correct denominator and is null if that denominator is zero. The B response partition is new gold
/ old gold / another legal answer / invalid or missing, with counts summing to the ﬁxed N.
Invariance.
ID-renamed answers are mapped to canonical identities. Choice letters are only comparable after mapping to
their option meanings. Exact sets ignore order while retaining duplicate-invalid status; action success uses legal execution. The
ﬁve transitions are both correct, A-only correct, B-only correct, both wrong with the same semantic answer, and both wrong
otherwise. Missing or invalid answers are explicitly identiﬁed and cannot count as valid semantic agreement.
Withdrawal and access.
Removing images or edges can make a task underdetermined. Scores retain original gold only
as dependency measurements. Full/Mask measures a total access effect, and ﬁxed two-view access is not active exploration.
Semantic invariance under camera or color changes does not prove visual decidability.
Weighting and uncertainty.
First average variants within each original parent, then parents within source, then sources
equally. Micro and parent-macro rates are auxiliary. Report items, parents and sources for every family and QA stratum.
Resample source clusters with replacement 10,000 times (seed 20260919), using percentile 95% intervals. Calculate model-
minus-baseline differences on the same records before resampling. No equivalence claim follows from an interval including
zero. Subgroups chosen after inspecting model performance must be marked exploratory.

the target. The candidate-label swap changes the image-conditioned answer from C to A. The paper enlarges the pixels; these
details are not additional inputs or human answerability approvals.
The Mini Backhoe Loader has 246 placed parts. B0035 is brick_000035, black part 32250, at source line 206 in 42004
- front right side.ldr. B0036 is yellow part 32250 at line 207. B0196 is a black 2780 pin from another submodel.
The options are A:B0196, B:B0116, C:B0036, D:B0060. The wrong-image render exchanges labels B0036 and B0196 while the
question and options remain ﬁxed. Thus the original gold C and new-image gold A describe different observation-conditioned
bindings, not contradictory claims about source CAD truth.
For this pair, C→A yields both-correct, C→C is old-answer persistence, C→B is another legal error, and a missing B is
a failure. These are hypothetical response examples. No model predictions have been observed. The same identity links rec-
ognized axle edges to B0034/B0037/B0038/B0051 and the independent restore-B0035 display action. Those are separate
contracts.

Challenge rules.
The extension version is ldraw2-matched-graphs-v1, seed 20260919. Each parent contributes three
hypothetical observations; the maximum per source is 15. A target-to-survivors star is combined with either a six-cycle/two-
triangle pair (at least six survivors) or a four-node path/three-node triangle pair (four or ﬁve survivors). Other survivors are
isolated after target deletion. The third observation permutes survivor labels in the anchor graph. Seeded parent ordering
alternates the direction of the answer change.
The 219 observations produce 146 pairs: 37 increases, 36 decreases and 73 unchanged answers. All match node set, unique
edge count and target degree. 12 changed pairs also match survivor degree sequences; 61 do not, and the manifest records that
gap. Edge counts need not match the original natural graph. Natural, single-edge intervention and hypothetical challenge results
remain separate. Opaque request IDs and independent requests do not expose pair membership or transformation direction to
the model.
Independent checks.
BFS and union-ﬁnd agree on every original, original-intervention and challenge graph. Python and the
frozen TypeScript intervention yield identical original-intervention edge lists. A synthetic six-cycle versus two-triangle ﬁxture
veriﬁes that node/edge counts and every survivor degree can agree while the component count differs. Duplicate, reversed and
isolated-edge cases are included. Synthetic ﬁxtures are software tests, never counted as real sources.
7

6
Related-work Evidence at Protocol Level
All comparisons use pinned full papers, read including appendices: BrickNet, CVPR 2026 proceedings, ten PDF pages; LEGO-
Puzzles arXiv:2503.19990v3, June 20, 2025, eleven pages; PhyBlock arXiv:2506.08708v2, November 21, 2025, forty pages.
Page numbers below refer to PDF pages. Ofﬁcial code snapshots are separately identiﬁed and are not substituted for the papers’
experimental recipes.
6.1
BrickNet
BrickNet [Kulits and Schmid(2026)] evaluates unconditional and caption-conditioned object/build-sequence generation and
held-out sequence perplexity (pp. 5–7, Sec. 5). Conditional generation receives a caption; unconditional generation receives a
start token. Graph-backed part/connector programs reconstruct poses (pp. 3–4, Sec. 3.4). Eight views are used for captioning
(p. 4), and render–text agreement evaluates generated objects (p. 7).
Its placed-part graph vertices and letter-named spanning-tree programs distinguish repeated instances (pp. 3–4, Fig. 4).
Connector annotations combine procedural and manual authoring and are reviewed; collision geometry uses inset meshes
with caveats about non-watertight surfaces and deformation (pp. 3–4). The paper uses human-authored online LDraw sources,
reporting 320,808 samples and 9,743 variants, with 512 held-out single-object evaluation ﬁles (pp. 2,4–5).
Graph/pose representation, sampling, scale and training ablations are reported (pp. 5–7). Same-target answer-changing and
answer-preserving VQA with the two semantic adaptation measures is not reported. This does not imply the assets could not
support that experiment. The code snapshot at commit b09a689c4929bd27f59c9bb9446237967aa332c9 (August 19,
2026) preserves instance/connector identity through core.Graph, core.Edge and tree.id2i. score.score_text
returns action count, ﬁrst invalid action and collision indices; score.check_tree decodes placements. The paper regener-
ates unparseable caption-conditioned outputs before perceptual scoring (p. 7), unlike our ﬁxed-denominator failure rule. Later
DATA.md training splits and packing lengths differ from the paper; those recipes are not merged in our comparison.
6.2
LEGO-Puzzles
LEGO-Puzzles [Tang et al.(2025)Tang, Gao, Zeng, Duan, Sun, Xing, Liu, Lyu, and Chen] v3 has 1,100 questions from 407
building instructions across eleven families (pp. 3–4). Task-dependent inputs include current/target states, next pieces and
candidate states (Fig. 1, p. 2; Sec. 3.2, p. 4). Height/Adjacency use arrows, while states and choices use letters. Uniﬁed source
naming is described (p. 4), but a stable scene-instance identiﬁer shared across image, record, graph and action contracts is
not reported in this version. Several tasks integrate multiple image sources; describing them all as independent single-image
lookups would be incorrect.
Studio ﬁxes cameras across steps, and type, number, color, position and viewpoint can be edited during construction (p. 4).
Duplicate/image checks and three-annotator veriﬁcation resolve or remove ambiguous items (p. 4). VQA scoring combines
rule-based extraction with a ChatGPT fallback; ﬁve experts rate image generation (pp. 5,7). Next-k-Step uses k = 1, . . . , 5 and
compares CoT conditions (pp. 7–8); the version has up to seven intermediate stages (pp. 3–4). We do not import counts from a
later OpenReview revision.
Ofﬁcial code at commit 1413d2c236879573cc0afff646bae5e3d04023bd (June 20, 2025) uses LEGO.split_
LEGO to interleave image tokens and build_prompt_sort for ordered letters. These builders supply images and text,
not curation CAD records. extract_answer_from_item calls can_infer_lego; an enabled judge can retry three
times and fall back to a random option after exhausted matching. This is a code-snapshot observation, not a rerun score. The
same-target new-gold and both-correct estimand is not reported in v3, although the assets are reusable.
6.3
PhyBlock
PhyBlock [Ma et al.(2025)Ma, Wen, Lin, Xu, Liang, Lin, Ma, Wang, Wei, Lin, Han, Cao, Chen, Laptev, and Liang] v2 sepa-
rates 400 assembly tasks from 2,200 physical VQA questions across sixteen categories (pp. 5–6). Planning uses target and
candidate-block images; interactive planning adds observations and history (pp. 7–8,25–27). The output selects reusable
candidate IDs in order without rotation; exact pose prediction is deferred (pp. 39–40). The evaluator uses AOV dependencies
and target poses.
Scene records have unique order, type, color, dependencies, position and orientation (pp. 17–18). Candidate IDs are
different: the prompt explicitly permits the same block index multiple times (pp. 25–26). Planning jointly involves candidate
matching and legal ordering (pp. 22–23), while VQA separately probes attributes, relations, scenes and dynamics (pp. 5–6).
Eight geometries and ﬁve colors are used with Genesis, manual scene annotation and augmentation (pp. 6,15–17).
AOV evaluation matches unused legal reference blocks and counts TP/FP/FN (Algorithm 1, pp. 22–23). Static VQA uses
human-veriﬁed LLM generation; dynamic VQA uses simulated perturbations, frames and human checks (p. 19). The paper
8

reports topology/pose scoring, full/stepwise planning, views/backgrounds and physical perturbations (pp. 8,19,22–28), with no
real-robot evaluation (p. 40). Our speciﬁc paired visual estimand is not reported.
The ofﬁcial commit da81ed9cad8184509aadb6f01af83d53e5c9d6fa (August 20, 2025) predates v2. Its sample
000.json conﬁrms order/depend/euler scene ﬁelds. are_blocks_equal compares type/color/euler in protocol
A and type/color in B. validate_block_placement assigns the earliest unused legal reference instance. extract_
blocks_sequence ﬁlters invalid candidate indices. BlockEnv.move_cube selects a candidate using a target posi-
tion/euler and step renders feedback. This is not continuous pose/control synthesis. The top-level evaluator does not sep-
arately emit all four paper error types.
What the additional assets establish.
IDs and templates can support attribute lookup on these prior assets. Our additional
unit is a bound pair: canonical target, exact visible evidence, both golds, truth policy, raw predictions and conditional answer-
ability decisions. It allows testing correct adaptation instead of mere score change. The natural graph audit demonstrates why
this distinction matters for the present distribution; the analogous visual ﬁnding remains a research objective. No exclusivity,
ﬁrstness or cross-benchmark percentage comparison is asserted.
9

7
Quality, Failure Handling and Software Validation
The required queue contains 140 visual items and 111 other ﬂagged items. The physical queue contains 573 intersection
candidates without recognized mating interfaces. Intended mating, approximate mesh overlap, source defects and unresolved
cases require separate decisions. All are pending human adjudication; an automated image or graph check does not resolve
them.
Exact-wire visual review records task, condition, request hash, image hashes/dimensions, reviewer, date, native-
size/blinding attestations and reason. A browser review export shows actual wire pixels without references. Pending/failed
QA is reported and cannot silently delete an unfavorable model result. Changing the input or gold requires a new version and
affected runs; old scores cannot be ported without veriﬁcation.
The adaptation test suite covers perfect adaptation with zero accuracy difference, persistent old answers, other legal answers,
invalid outputs, a zero conditional denominator, mismatched models/data, duplicate pairs and missing B observations. Raw-
receipt ﬁxtures exercise all eleven families, including executable actions. Tampered request bindings, numeric metrics, counts,
states and hashes are rejected. Synthetic receipts are explicitly labeled and rejected by production ingestion. These passing
tests demonstrate analysis behavior, not model performance or human agreement.
Missing, timed-out, refused, invalid-JSON and invalid-format responses retain distinct reasons and count as failures in the
primary denominator. Format validity and semantic accuracy conditional on valid format are auxiliary. No hidden retries or
answer repair inﬂate the main rate. Unknown cost remains null.
8
Reproduction, Preservation and Figure Provenance
The pre-revision archive preserves 2,616 actual ﬁle entries, including previously untracked content. Its baseline lock checks all
four guide ﬁngerprints and the earlier 2,131-ﬁle v1 freeze. The new namespace contains analysis code, result indices, paired
gold bindings and the hypothetical extension; no original source, v1/v2 request, response, gold, score or manuscript is rewritten.
The publication generator reads hash-checked frozen inputs, produces numeric macros and imports the state-driven tables.
It records every source render and paper crop. The ofﬁcial CVPR 2026 style is copied unchanged and used in anonymous
review mode with a nonregistered draft ID. Source archives include TeX, bibliography, ﬁgure PDFs, complete dossiers and
hashes. Local build/veriﬁcation instructions are supplied with the release; machine-speciﬁc paths are not part of this PDF.
All ﬁgure images are derivatives of real source geometry renders. Paper-only crop rectangles and zooms never change
model inputs. The graph drawing is explicitly schematic; state arrows show visibility effects, not physical motion. Six-step
images use one shared rectangle from unchanged saved frames. Full/Mask share the exact camera, projection and crop window;
Crop reframes operands. No additional artwork substitutes for a source model.

Detailed Family Contracts
9.1
Color
73 items; visual; atomic; single-choice.
Public input.
{}; visualInput.numberedView supplies a frozen operand-isolation PNG; isolationAllowed=true
Operation and scoring.
Bind the target B-number in the image; identify its source color name. Exact anonymous choiceId
of the semantic answer.
Interpretation.
Source color equality is an oracle, not proof of perceptual distinguishability.
Control.
No known deterministic S1–S4 request shortcut; semantic priors reported separately.
9.2
Part type
67 items; visual; atomic; single-choice.
Public input.
{}; visualInput.numberedView supplies a frozen operand-isolation PNG; isolationAllowed=true
10

Operation and scoring.
Bind target and candidates in the image; match the source part type while ignoring color and pose.
Exact anonymous choiceId of the semantic answer.
Interpretation.
Near-identical source types require human adjudication; no unseen geometry is inferred.
Control.
No known deterministic S1–S4 request shortcut; semantic priors reported separately.
9.3
Distance
73 items; source-data; atomic; single-choice.
Public input.
centers, coordinateFrame
Operation and scoring.
Read supplied bounding-box centers, compute Euclidean distance, select the unique minimum. Ex-
act anonymous choiceId of the semantic answer.
Interpretation.
Coordinates are explicit, not visually estimated. Candidates are hash sampled, not nearest spatial neighbors.
Control.
No known deterministic S1–S4 request shortcut; semantic priors reported separately.

connectorRecord.{family,aConnector,bConnector}; scope
Operation and scoring.
Read connectorRecord.family and select the identical label. Exact anonymous choiceId of the
semantic answer.
Interpretation.
An explicit record-reading control, not contact perception or load-capacity reasoning.
Control.
No known deterministic S1–S4 request shortcut; semantic priors reported separately.
9.5
Neighbors
73 items; connector-graph; metacognitive; multiple-choice.
Public input.
edges, graphMeaning
Operation and scoring.
Collect candidate labels joined to the target by any supplied undirected edge; deduplicate. Exact set
of choiceIds; order ignored; duplicate IDs invalid.
Interpretation.
One-hop set extraction within the recognized graph, not physical connectivity discovery.
Control.
No known deterministic S1–S4 request shortcut; semantic priors reported separately.
9.6
Graph deletion
73 items; connector-graph; graph-internal; integer.
Public input.
edges, graphMeaning, nodes
Operation and scoring.
Delete the named vertex and incident edges; count connected components including remaining
isolated vertices. A safe JSON integer value exactly equal to the independent component count.

Interpretation.
An operation within a supplied local graph; no extraction trajectory or cross-contract chain.
Control.
No known deterministic S1–S4 request shortcut; semantic priors reported separately.

9.9
Step lookup
55 items; source-data; procedural; single-choice.
Public input.
steps[].{index,numbers}; provenance
Operation and scoring.
Find the target label in the supplied author-step table; select the smallest matching expanded index.
Exact anonymous choiceId of the semantic answer.
Interpretation.
Table lookup; source order is not evidence of collision-free assembly.
Control.
No known deterministic S1–S4 request shortcut; semantic priors reported separately.
9.10
Step sequence
17 items; scene-edit; procedural; actions.
Public input.
initialFacts; goalFacts; absentFacts; budget; actions[].{id,label,requires,forbids?,adds,deletes,cost}; editMode
Operation and scoring.
Follow the public predecessor facts and reveal the supplied author window in order. String actionIds;
legal full replay under prerequisites, forbids and budget; all goal facts and no prohibited facts.
Interpretation.
A small explicit fact chain, not mechanical planning; the current six-step response template is constant.
12

Control.
No known deterministic S1–S4 request shortcut; semantic priors reported separately. Identical six-step action pro-
gram in this release.
9.11
Restoration
58 items; scene-edit; procedural; actions.
Public input.
initialFacts; goalFacts; absentFacts; budget; actions[].{id,label,requires,forbids?,adds,deletes,cost}; editMode
Operation and scoring.
Choose the restore action whose missing fact holds; execute it within budget and satisfy all goals.
String actionIds; legal full replay under prerequisites, forbids and budget; all goal facts and no prohibited facts.
Interpretation.
One visibility edit at an original source pose, not insertion-feasibility planning.
Control.
No known deterministic S1–S4 request shortcut; semantic priors reported separately.
